### Much Ado About Nothing
**Event ID:** 8640  
**Start:** 2027-03-20 19:30  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
Beatrice and Benedick are not in love. Ask them, they’ll tell you. Loudly. Repeatedly. With increasingly elaborate insults. The only people who aren’t buying it? Everyone else. Shakespeare’s wittiest comedy throws two of the sharpest tongues in theater onto a collision course while their friends scheme and meddle to force what everyone already knows. But the insults are getting softer. The glances are lasting longer. And pride is the only thing still standing in the way.
```

**Event ID:** 8769  
**Start:** 2026-10-03 19:30

### Holst’s The Planets
**Event ID:** 8740  
**Start:** 2026-10-08 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Oregon Symphony](https://ode.fm/organizer/oregon-symphony)  
**Category:** Symphony  
**Location:** Arlene Schnitzer Concert Hall  
**Address:** 1037 SW Broadway, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
Take a tour of the Solar System with Holst’s popular The Planets, featuring seven orchestral fantasies that range from warlike “Mars” to mystic “Neptune.” The suite joins Saariaho’s Asteroid 4179: Toutatis, inspired by the asteroid whose orbit passes closest to the Earth. Gabriela Lena Frank’s Picaflor: A future myth, in its West Coast premiere, draws on the mythology of Andean Perú to evoke the sky kingdom, its sun god, and its mischievous hummingbird (picaflor).

### Holst’s The Planets
**Event ID:** 8741  
**Start:** 2026-10-10 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Oregon Symphony](https://ode.fm/organizer/oregon-symphony)  
**Category:** Symphony  
**Location:** Arlene Schnitzer Concert Hall  
**Address:** 1037 SW Broadway, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
Take a tour of the Solar System with Holst’s popular The Planets, featuring seven orchestral fantasies that range from warlike “Mars” to mystic “Neptune.” The suite joins Saariaho’s Asteroid 4179: Toutatis, inspired by the asteroid whose orbit passes closest to the Earth. Gabriela Lena Frank’s Picaflor: A future myth, in its West Coast premiere, draws on the mythology of Andean Perú to evoke the sky kingdom, its sun god, and its mischievous hummingbird (picaflor).

The least scary thing you’ll see this Halloween season

Leonardo is a terrible monster. He tries so hard to be scary, but he just…isn’t. But then he hatches a plan to find the most scaredy-cat kid in the world. Will Leonardo finally get to scare someone? Or will it be the start of an unlikely friendship?

This Halloween season, join Seattle Children’s Theatre for a not-very-spine-chilling, not-particularly-hair-raising, not-very-frightening-at-all-really romp with a monster. Based on the book by beloved author Mo Willems, Leonardo! A Wonderful [...]
```

### I’ll Be Your Superhero
**Event ID:** 8827  
**Start:** 2026-10-24 14:00  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Seattle Children's Theatre](https://ode.fm/organizer/seattle-children-s-theatre)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Tags:** family friendly  
**Location:** Seattle Children's Theatre  
**Address:** 201 Thomas St, Seattle, WA 98109  
**Description:**
```
Can becoming someone else help you discover who you are?

When a 10-year-old boy named Xavier has to go live with his uncle, it isn’t easy. His uncle John John has just left the Marines after eight years of service, and both must navigate family issues and strong emotions—both within themselves and with each other.

But something happens when they hear about the most famous Comic Con in the world. Xavier and John John discover a mutual love of superheroes and decide to take the trip, donning their own costumes. Can a superhero outfit help them [...]
```

### Table 17
**Event ID:** 8582  
**Start:** 2026-10-24 19:30  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
If your ex called, would you answer? Jada and Dallas were once engaged. Now, two years after their breakup, they’re sitting across from each other at their favorite restaurant, trying to keep it casual. Good luck with that. Between a waiter with no boundaries, wounds that never quite healed, and a spark that neither one of them can ignore, this “simple dinner” is about to go off script. From two-time Emmy nominee and playwright Douglas Lyons, Table 17 is the best date night you’ll have all season. Grab your person and make a reservation
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/table-17-20260707063010-b05bd4bc.webp

### Kahane, Still & Copland
**Event ID:** 8774  
**Start:** 2026-10-24 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Oregon Symphony](https://ode.fm/organizer/oregon-symphony)  
**Category:** Symphony  
**Location:** Arlene Schnitzer Concert Hall  
**Address:** 1037 SW Broadway, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
Still’s “Autochthonous” Symphony No. 4, a radiantly lyrical tribute to those who have triumphed over a difficult past, honors the American people’s optimism and dreams of hope. Copland’s Lincoln Portrait highlights the 16th president’s character and thoughts in music threaded with selections from his celebrated writings. A new work by Portland musician, writer, and storyteller Gabriel Kahane rounds out this program of modern Americana.
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/kahane-still-copland-20260717210924-f93703c5.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8774/kahane-still-copland-portland-october-24-2026

### Psycho in Concert
**Event ID:** 8776  
**Start:** 2026-10-28 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Oregon Symphony](https://ode.fm/organizer/oregon-symphony)  
**Category:** Symphony  
**Tags:** film score  
**Location:** Arlene Schnitzer Concert Hall  
**Address:** 1037 SW Broadway, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
A secretary on the run takes refuge at a secluded California motel where she meets Norman Bates, a repressed man under the influence of his overbearing mother. Get in the Halloween spirit with this iconic Alfred Hitchcock thriller, live in concert! The Oregon Symphony’s brilliant string section will perform Bernard Herrmann’s beloved score live-to-picture, bringing a palpable tension to the film that takes the already unsettling story to a new level of terror.
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/psycho-in-concert-20260717210933-5222a965.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8776/psycho-in-concert-portland-october-28-2026

**Start:** 2026-10-31 19:30  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
If your ex called, would you answer? Jada and Dallas were once engaged. Now, two years after their breakup, they’re sitting across from each other at their favorite restaurant, trying to keep it casual. Good luck with that. Between a waiter with no boundaries, wounds that never quite healed, and a spark that neither one of them can ignore, this “simple dinner” is about to go off script. From two-time Emmy nominee and playwright Douglas Lyons, Table 17 is the best date night you’ll have all season. Grab your person and make a reservation
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/table-17-20260707063010-b05bd4bc.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8589/table-17-seattle-october-31-2026

### Stravinsky & Beethoven
**Event ID:** 8777  
**Start:** 2026-10-31 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Oregon Symphony](https://ode.fm/organizer/oregon-symphony)  
**Category:** Symphony  
**Location:** Arlene Schnitzer Concert Hall  
**Address:** 1037 SW Broadway, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
Inspired by Crater Lake National Park, Michael Gordon’s Natural History weaves Native and symphonic music in this paean to the natural landscape. Plus, hear works by two of classical music’s most intrepid pioneers: Stravinsky’s experimental musical essay on wind and brass sounds, Symphonies of Wind Instruments, and Beethoven’s vivacious and dramatic Symphony No. 1. Native Oregonian Grace Miedziak enhances the program with a new work that celebrates the Pacific Northwest.
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/stravinsky-beethoven-20260717210926-08fc9fee.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8777/stravinsky-beethoven-portland-october-31-2026  
---
### Leonardo! A Wonderful Show About a Terrible Monster

**Start:** 2026-11-01 10:00

**Address:** 201 Thomas St, Seattle, WA 98109  
**Description:**
```
The least scary thing you’ll see this Halloween season

Leonardo is a terrible monster. He tries so hard to be scary, but he just…isn’t. But then he hatches a plan to find the most scaredy-cat kid in the world. Will Leonardo finally get to scare someone? Or will it be the start of an unlikely friendship?

This Halloween season, join Seattle Children’s Theatre for a not-very-spine-chilling, not-particularly-hair-raising, not-very-frightening-at-all-really romp with a monster. Based on the book by beloved author Mo Willems, Leonardo! A Wonderful [...]
```

### I’ll Be Your Superhero
**Event ID:** 8834

```
Can becoming someone else help you discover who you are?

When a 10-year-old boy named Xavier has to go live with his uncle, it isn’t easy. His uncle John John has just left the Marines after eight years of service, and both must navigate family issues and strong emotions—both within themselves and with each other.

But something happens when they hear about the most famous Comic Con in the world. Xavier and John John discover a mutual love of superheroes and decide to take the trip, donning their own costumes. Can a superhero outfit help them [...]

### A Streetcar Named Desire
**Event ID:** 8609  
**Start:** 2026-11-05 19:30  
**City:** Boston (`bos`)  
**Organizer:** [Boston Lyric Opera](https://ode.fm/organizer/boston-lyric-opera)  
**Category:** Opera  
**Location:** Emerson Colonial Theatre  
**Address:** 106 Boylston Street, Boston, MA 02116  
**Description:**
```
“Real? Who wants real?… I want magic!”

The sweltering heat of a New Orleans summer sets the tone for the simmering tensions that crackle and spark between the unforgettable characters of Tennessee Williams’ Pulitzer Prize-winning play, set to André Previn’s luminous score. Magnetic but self-deluded Blanche DuBois leaves her Mississippi hometown and seeks shelter with her sister Stella and her husband Stanley. Clinging to a romanticized image of the past, she quickly finds herself at odds with the domineering Stanley, whose brutal realism [...]
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/a-streetcar-named-desire-20260709201627-24d7b05e.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8609/a-streetcar-named-desire-boston-november-5-2026

### Literary Arts Book Fest
**Event ID:** 8779  
**Start:** 2026-11-06 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [45th Parallel](https://ode.fm/organizer/45th-parallel)  
**Category:** Chamber  
**Location:** Sanctuary Hall at First Congregational Church  
**Address:** 1126 SW Park Ave, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
As part of the Literary Arts Book Fest, 45th Parallel Universe joins local musicians who are also writers to embark on a unique concert experience.

Music is more than just an aural language - it is a multitude of communication modes and literacies. Sight Reading features world-renowned songwriters translating between dialects in real time: the written language of music (usually expressed on staff), oral / aural traditions and “playing by ear” come together to work with 45th Parallel Universe musicians as they perform newly-created [...]
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/literary-arts-book-fest-20260717211409-eda02a7c.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8779/literary-arts-book-fest-portland-november-6-2026

### Table 17
**Event ID:** 8603  
**Start:** 2026-11-14 14:00  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Tags:** ASL service, audio described, daytime  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
If your ex called, would you answer? Jada and Dallas were once engaged. Now, two years after their breakup, they’re sitting across from each other at their favorite restaurant, trying to keep it casual. Good luck with that. Between a waiter with no boundaries, wounds that never quite healed, and a spark that neither one of them can ignore, this “simple dinner” is about to go off script. From two-time Emmy nominee and playwright Douglas Lyons, Table 17 is the best date night you’ll have all season. Grab your person and make a reservation
```

### Friends of Chamber Music presents Hamilton & Friends
**Event ID:** 8759  
**Start:** 2026-11-15 19:00  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Friends of Chamber Music](https://ode.fm/organizer/friends-of-chamber-music)  
**Category:** Chamber  
**Location:** The Old Church  
**Address:** 1422 SW 11th Ave, Portland, OR 97201  
**Description:**
```
Join us for two special programs with Hamilton Cheifetz and friends (Sept 27th & Nov 15th) featuring some of the finest musicians from our community. Admission is “pay what you will” with a portion of the proceeds going to support the String Ensemble Community Outreach program which features PSU music students in performances at local prisons.

Guest artists on September 27 include Jessica Lee (violin), Greg Ewer (violin), Kenji Bunch (viola), James Shields (clarinet), Julia Hwakyu Lee (piano), and Hamilton Cheifetz (cello)

### Shostakovich & Rachmaninoff
**Event ID:** 8746  
**Start:** 2027-01-07 19:30  
**City:** Portland (`pdx`)  
**Organizer:** [Oregon Symphony](https://ode.fm/organizer/oregon-symphony)  
**Category:** Symphony  
**Location:** Arlene Schnitzer Concert Hall  
**Address:** 1037 SW Broadway, Portland, OR 97205  
**Description:**
```
Strife and battle erupt in Shostakovich’s musical portrayal of the precursor to the Russian Revolution in his Symphony No. 11, “The Year 1905”. Superstar pianist Marc-André Hamelin joins the Symphony in Rachmaninoff’s Rhapsody on a Theme of Paganini, an exhilarating joyride of keyboard pyrotechnics, melodic passion, and humor. Plus, hear South Korean composer Donghoon Shin’s Kafka’s Dream, inspired by a colorfully surreal story by Jorge Luis Borges.
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/shostakovich-rachmaninoff-20260717204229-262528b4.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8746/shostakovich-rachmaninoff-portland-january-7-2027

### Twelve Kinds of Ice
**Event ID:** 8882  
**Start:** 2027-01-29 18:00  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Seattle Children's Theatre](https://ode.fm/organizer/seattle-children-s-theatre)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Tags:** family friendly  
**Location:** Seattle Children's Theatre  
**Address:** 201 Thomas St, Seattle, WA 98109  
**Description:**
```
The coldest season can bring the warmest memories

Young Ellen can’t wait for winter. From skating on frozen streams to parties on an icy lake, winter brought friends, family, and fun together, creating memories that never leave her.

In this cozy wintertime play at Seattle Children’s Theatre, based on the award-winning book by Ellen Bryan Obed, Twelve Kinds of Ice sees an adult Ellen sharing stories from her winters in Maine and how she learned that anything is possible with practice and care. Featuring live music on piano and guitar, Ellen [...]

### Meteor Shower
**Event ID:** 8616  
**Start:** 2027-02-06 19:30  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
Leave it to Steve Martin to turn a quiet evening under the stars into total, glorious chaos. Corky and Norm have invited another couple over to watch a once-in-a-lifetime meteor shower. Simple enough. But something about the guests is… off, the conversation keeps taking sharp turns, and somewhere between the wine and the sky falling, the line between what’s real and what’s not dissolves completely. This is Steve Martin at his most inventive: a comedy that goes exactly where you don’t expect and never once looks back.
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/meteor-shower-20260709201730-c5116f8f.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8616/meteor-shower-seattle-february-6-2027

Beneath the story’s light-hearted surface is a skillful and nuanced [...]

### Much Ado About Nothing
**Event ID:** 8640  
**Start:** 2027-03-20 19:30  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
Beatrice and Benedick are not in love. Ask them, they’ll tell you. Loudly. Repeatedly. With increasingly elaborate insults. The only people who aren’t buying it? Everyone else. Shakespeare’s wittiest comedy throws two of the sharpest tongues in theater onto a collision course while their friends scheme and meddle to force what everyone already knows. But the insults are getting softer. The glances are lasting longer. And pride is the only thing still standing in the way.
```

**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
Beatrice and Benedick are not in love. Ask them, they’ll tell you. Loudly. Repeatedly. With increasingly elaborate insults. The only people who aren’t buying it? Everyone else. Shakespeare’s wittiest comedy throws two of the sharpest tongues in theater onto a collision course while their friends scheme and meddle to force what everyone already knows. But the insults are getting softer. The glances are lasting longer. And pride is the only thing still standing in the way.
```

### The Gruffalo
**Event ID:** 8935  
**Start:** 2027-04-22 18:00  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Seattle Children's Theatre](https://ode.fm/organizer/seattle-children-s-theatre)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Tags:** family friendly  
**Location:** Seattle Center  
**Address:** 305 Harrison St, Seattle, WA 98109  
**Description:**
```
Where are you going to, little brown mouse?

Join Mouse on a daring adventure through the deep, dark wood in Seattle Children’s Theatre’s magical, musical adaptation of the classic picture book by Julia Donaldson & Axel Scheffler.

Searching for hazelnuts, Mouse meets the cunning Fox, the eccentric old Owl, and the high-spirited Snake. Will the story of the terrifying Gruffalo save Mouse from ending up as dinner for these hungry woodland creatures? After all, there’s no such thing as a Gruffalo—is there?
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/the-gruffalo-20260717220022-0921e9d6.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8935/the-gruffalo-seattle-april-22-2027

### The River Bride
**Event ID:** 8664  
**Start:** 2027-05-01 19:30  
**City:** Seattle (`sea`)  
**Organizer:** [Union Arts Center](https://ode.fm/organizer/union-arts-center)  
**Category:** Theater  
**Location:** Union Arts Center  
**Address:** 700 Union St, Seattle, WA 98101  
**Description:**
```
Three days before her sister’s wedding, a stranger is pulled from the Amazon River. No name. No memory. No explanation. Just a magnetism that neither sister can resist. In a small Brazilian fishing village where old stories still hold power, Marisela Treviño Orta weaves a modern fable of desire, jealousy, and the ties that bind us to the people we love and the ones we should have chosen. Part folklore, part love story, part fairy tale with teeth, The River Bride asks: when fate pulls something extraordinary out of the water, do you trust it?
```
**Event Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/eventimg/the-river-bride-20260709202255-772d69ff.webp  
**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8664/the-river-bride-seattle-may-1-2027

In a small Brazilian fishing village where old stories still hold power, Marisela Treviño Orta weaves a modern fable of desire, jealousy, and the ties that bind us to the people we love and the ones we should have chosen. Part folklore, part love story, part fairy tale with teeth, The River Bride asks: when fate pulls something extraordinary out of the water, do you trust it?
```

Three days before her sister’s wedding, a stranger is pulled from the Amazon River. No name. No memory. No explanation. Just a magnetism that neither sister can resist. In a small Brazilian fishing village where old stories still hold power, Marisela Treviño Orta weaves a modern fable of desire, jealousy, and the ties that bind us to the people we love and the ones we should have chosen. Part folklore, part love story, part fairy tale with teeth, The River Bride asks: when fate pulls something extraordinary out of the water, do you trust it?
```

**Event Page:** https://ode.fm/event/8682/the-river-bride-seattle-may-20-2027

### Ayre

A living tapestry of cultures in conversation.

Featuring sung and spoken texts in Ladino, Arabic, Hebrew, Spanish, Sardinian, and English in a production that physicalizes intimacy [...]

**Description:**
```
We are a collective of musicians who come together to celebrate great chamber music with intimate artistic experiences. Passion for great music is what defines us and Portland is our stage. Since 2009, 45th Parallel has happily demolished distinctions between old and new chamber music, bluegrass and jazz, fiddle and folk. By bringing fragmented audiences together, 45th Parallel reflects Portland’s surging creative communities in fresh and imaginative ways. Founded by Greg Ewer, a violinist of insatiable curiosity, 45th Parallel stands apart from the city’s other chamber groups by its borderles…

### A Far Cry Chamber Orchestra

[Website](https://www.afarcry.org/)  
**City:** Boston  
**Description:**
```
Described as offering “a refreshing corrective to classical music’s rigid hierarchies” by WBUR, A Far Cry is known for its innovative and compelling programs, stellar communication, and a democratic process that is the group’s north star. A Far Cry was founded in 2007 on the belief that every voice deserves to be heard; so instead of one artistic director, the collective of musicians (“Criers”) curate and submit program ideas inspired by individual curiosities, and what is happening in the world at large. The result is ensemble playing at its most intimate, representing a diverse mix of music …
```

### American Repertory Theater
[Organizer Page](https://ode.fm/organizer/american-repertory-theater)  
[Website](https://americanrepertorytheater.org/)  
**City:** Boston  
**Description:**
```
The American Repertory Theater (A.R.T.) at Harvard University is a leading force in the American theater, producing groundbreaking work that is driven by risk-taking and passionate inquiry. A.R.T. was founded in 1980 by Robert Brustein, who served as Artistic Director until 2002, when he was succeeded by Robert Woodruff. Diane Paulus began her tenure as Terrie and Bradley Bloom Artistic Director in 2008 and co-leads the theater in partnership with Executive Director Kelvin Dinkins, Jr., who began his tenure in June 2022.
```

Situated on the traditional, ancestral, and unceded territories of the xʷməθkʷəy̓əm (Musqueam), Sḵwx̱wú7mesh (Squamish), and Sel̓íl̓witulh (Tsleil-Waututh) nations, the Arts Club Theatre Company is the largest theatre company in Western Canada. ### Arts On

Arts On is where community and culture converge in fresh, engaging ways. Art brings people together, sparks conversation, and creates meaningful shared experiences. It highlights diverse voices and perspectives, encourages exploration and experimentation, and celebrates creativity in all its forms. Through performances, exhibitions, and programs, Arts On connects people with ideas, stories, and moments that inspire, challenge, and entertain, enriching the cultural life of the community.
```

### Boston Public Library
[Organizer Page](https://ode.fm/organizer/boston-public-library)  
[Website](https://www.bpl.org/)  
**City:** Boston  
**Description:**
```
Established in 1848, the Boston Public Library is a pioneer of public library service in America. It was the first large free municipal library in the United States, the first public library to lend books, the first to have a branch library, and the first to have a children’s room. As a City of Boston historic cultural institution, Boston Public Library today features a central library and twenty-five neighborhood branches, serving nearly 4 million visitors per year and millions more online. Boston Public Library is a department of the City of Boston, under the leadership of Mayor Michelle Wu.…
```

[Website](https://www.citizensoperahouse.com/)  
**City:** Boston  
**Description:**
```
The Citizens Opera House is one of the finest examples of the vaudeville circuit palace at the pinnacle of its development. Designed in a combination of French and Italian styles by Thomas White Lamb, one of the foremost theatre architects of his day, it was erected under the close personal supervision of Edward Franklin Albee to memorialize his late partner, Benjamin Franklin Keith. Because it was constructed as a memorial and tribute to vaudeville’s greatest impresario, it was built with a degree of luxury in its appointments that is almost unrivaled. A Historic Theatrical Masterpiece Restor…
```

### Fulcrum Point New Music
[Organizer Page](https://ode.fm/organizer/fulcrum-point-new-music)  
[Website](https://www.fulcrumpoint.org/)  
**City:** Chicago  
**Description:**
```
Chicago has a formidable reputation as a creative incubator for theater, architecture, and design. When Fulcrum Point New Music Project was established in 1998, Chicago’s new music scene was narrowly focused in contemporary classical music and stilted in its accepted range of styles. No group was exploring the complete spectrum of new musical possibility. Dominated by the academic contemporary canon, composers in genres such as minimalism, neo-classicism, neo-romanticism, postmodernism, and multimedia art had few outlets for expression and resources for support.
```

### Gallery Concerts
[Organizer Page](https://ode.fm/organizer/gallery-concerts)  
[Website](http://galleryconcerts.org)  
**City:** Seattle  
**Description:**
```
Gallery Concerts is dedicated to presenting chamber music of the 17th, 18th and early 19th centuries performed by top musicians on period instruments, in historically informed styles and appropriate acoustic spaces. Jillon Stoppels Dupree and George Bozarth co-founded Gallery Concerts over thirty years ago. The first few seasons were under the umbrella of Seattle Early Music Guild (now Early Music Seattle), then incorporated as an independent 501-c-3 non-profit corporation in 1992. The first venues were painting galleries, and we have hosted our events in many different venues over the years.
```

[Website](https://www.portlandchamberorchestra.org/)  
**City:** Portland  
**Description:**
```
The oldest chamber ochestra in the United States, this ensemble focuses on bringing unique and innovative programing to the Pacific Northwest. The Portland Chamber Orchestra was founded in 1947 by Finnish composer and conductor, Boris Sirpo, who came to Portland after leaving his war-torn native country. The orchestra was originally comprised of selected music students at Lewis and Clark College where he was a faculty member. Now one of America’s oldest chamber orchestras, the commitment to quality performances of innovative repertoire that was instilled from the beginning continues.

### Bond Chapel
**Location ID:** 282  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/bond-chapel)

**Address:**
```
1025 E 58th St, Chicago, IL 60637
```
**Description:**
```
The Joseph Bond Chapel is connected to Swift Hall, home of the Divinity School, by a peaceful stone cloister. The chapel contains a bronze plaque designed by the famous sculptor Lorado Taft that was originally donated by Mrs. Joseph Bond in memory of her husband, who served as a trustee for the Baptist Theological Union (predecessor of the Divinity School). The inscription, which Mrs. Bond composed, reads: “Erected in Memory of Joseph Bond for the Worship of God and the Service of Man, 1926.” A section of the wood paneling near the tablet has been carved to read “Peace I Leave With You.”

### Britannia Shipyards Museum
**Location ID:** 176  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/britannia-shipyards-museum)

**Address:**
```
5180 Westwater Dr, Richmond, BC V7E 6P3, Canada
```
**Description:**
```
Britannia Shipyards is an authentic representation of a once thriving community of canneries, boat yards and residences. Many of the buildings date back to 1885 and tell the stories of multi-ethnic residents and workers at the Britannia Cannery and Britannia Shipyards: Chinese, European, First Nations and Japanese. This collection of buildings has national value and was designated a National Historic Site in 1991 by the National Historic Sites and Monuments Board.

### Cathedral Church of St. Paul
**Location ID:** 264  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/cathedral-church-of-st-paul)

**Address:**
```
138 Tremont St, Boston, MA 02111
```
**Description:**
```
The first example of Greek Revival architecture in Boston, St. Paul’s was a strong contrast to the colonial “meeting house” appearance of the Park Street Church (1809) across Tremont Street. The light Quincy granite, used for the body of the building, was brought from the quarries on the first railroad operated in the United States. The Ionic columns on the portico are of brown sandstone quarried from the region of Acquia Creek in Stafford County, Virginia.
```

### Citizens Opera House
**Location ID:** 75  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/citizens-opera-house)

**Address:**
```
539 Washington Street, Boston, MA 02111
```
**Description:**
```
The Citizens Opera House is one of the finest examples of the vaudeville circuit palace at the pinnacle of its development. Designed in a combination of French and Italian styles by Thomas White Lamb, one of the foremost theatre architects of his day, it was erected under the close personal supervision of Edward Franklin Albee to memorialize his late partner, Benjamin Franklin Keith. Because it was constructed as a memorial and tribute to vaudeville’s greatest impresario, it was built with a degree of luxury in its appointments that is almost unrivaled.
```

[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/miner-auditorium-at-sfjazz-center)

**Address:**
```
201 Franklin St, San Francisco, CA 94102
```
**Description:**
```
Opened in 2013, the 35,000-square-foot SFJAZZ Center is located on the corner of Fell and Franklin Streets in the heart of San Francisco’s fine arts district. The Center is the first stand-alone structure in the country built specifically for jazz performance, with the 700-seat Robert N. Miner Auditorium and 100-seat Joe Henderson Lab. The building was designed by award winning architect Mark Cavagnero with the audio designed by Sam Berkow, one of the nation’s premier acoustical consultants.

55 Music Concourse Dr, San Francisco, CA 94118

```

### Neptune Theatre
**Location ID:** 152  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/neptune-theatre)

**Address:**
```
1303 NE 45th St, Seattle, WA 98105
```
**Description:**
```
The U-Neptune Theatre was a magnificent playhouse located in the University District designed for motion picture entertainment when it opened in 1921. The theatre’s artistic taupe and blue nautical décor depicted many sea scenes reflecting its namesake, King Neptune. It remains an example of the classic suburban theatre architecture from a bygone era. In 2011 the Seattle Theatre Group® transformed the Neptune from a single screen movie house into a performing arts destination while maintaining the historic architectural integrity of the theatre.
```

### Orpheum
**Location ID:** 156  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/orpheum)

**Address:**
```
601 Smithe St, Vancouver, BC V6B 1L7, Canada
```
**Description:**
```
Grand, elegant and effortlessly dazzling, the Orpheum has entranced millions of people since opening in 1927. The domed auditorium is adorned with a sprawling ceiling mural, and crowned by a giant crystal chandelier. The Orpheum’s exquisite décor and sweeping staircases were designed in the rare Spanish Baroque Revival style and the magic and history of the space envelopes both audiences and performers. The theatre’s seats are arranged in curved arcs to ensure each patron benefits from an unobstructed view.
```

### Orpheum Theatre
**Location ID:** 199  
[Location Page](https://ode.fm/location/orpheum-theatre)

**Address:**
```
1192 Market St, San Francisco, CA 94102
```
**Description:**
```
Officially designated as a San Francisco Landmark in 1977 by the San Francisco Landmarks Preservation Advisory Board, the Orpheum Theatre originally opened in 1926 with an façade patterned after a 12th-century French cathedral. Although this 2,200-seat theatre—located at the corner of Market Street and 8th Street—saw an external rejuvenation in 2016, the original and historical integrity of the landmark prevails as the Orpheum continues to welcome audiences across the San Francisco Bay Area to revel in the razzle-dazzle of Broadway.
```

### World Forestry Center

**Address:**
```
4033 SW Canyon Rd, Portland, OR 97221
```
**Description:**
```
Located in Portland, Oregon, USA, the roots of World Forestry Center date back to the 1905 Lewis and Clark Exposition in which a massive log structure was built to house the Forestry Building. This giant “log cabin” was constructed with whole diameter logs on the scale of the Parthenon in Greece and served as a showcase of the timber industry until it was lost in a fire in 1964.
```

## User Features

*Ode provides personalized features for registered users to enhance their cultural experience.*

### Bookmarking Events
Users can save events to their personal collection for easy access and planning.

- **Feature:** Bookmark events with one click
- **Access:** View all saved events on profile bookmarks page
- **Sorting:** Sort by event date or recently saved

### Following Organizers
Users can follow arts organizations to stay updated on new events.

- **Feature:** Follow button on organizer pages
- **Access:** View followed organizers on profile following page
- **Notifications:** Receive alerts when followed organizers add new events

### Event Alerts & Notifications
Users receive notifications about events from organizations they follow.

- **Feature:** Real-time alerts for new events
- **Display:** Notification bell in header shows unread count
- **Management:** Track which alerts have been read

### Calendar Integration
Users can subscribe to their bookmarked events via calendar feed.

- **Feature:** iCal/ICS feed for saved events
- **Security:** Personal token-based authentication
- **Compatibility:** Works with Google Calendar, Apple Calendar, Outlook
- **Access:** Calendar subscribe button on bookmarks page

### Profile Management
Users can customize their account and preferences.

- **Features:** Profile picture upload, password management, email preferences
- **Account:** Secure authentication with 30-day session tokens

---

**Summary:**
```
A century after the death of architect Antoni Gaudí, whose fantastical buildings helped define Barcelona's cultural identity, his work is inspiring a new tribute in music. On June 10, Boston-based Catalan composer Olivia Pérez-Collellmir will premiere Seven Dreams of Gaudí, a symphonic-choral work honoring the architect's life and legacy. The performance at the Palau de la Música Catalana, Barcelona's ornate concert hall, is part of a year long public celebration of the Catalonian icon. "Gaudí's name represents the Catalan culture," she said, "our heritage, values, identity, history, language …

### A Case for the Architecture of Attention
**Publish Date:** 2025-12-23  
**Byline:** adam goehrig-bawany  
**Article Image:** https://r2-obj-storage.ode.fm/articleimg/kissa-headline-02.jpg
[Read Article](https://ode.fm/article/a-case-for-the-architecture-of-attention-jazz-kissa)

**Summary:**
```
It is harder than ever to hear anything on purpose. Music follows us everywhere, dissolved into playlists calibrated for productivity, sleep, focus, or the gym. It fills retail spaces and hotel lobbies as a ubiquitous utility that exists primarily to modulate the mood of a room. Listening has become frictionless and solitary, functioning as a backdrop rather than a central event. We have gained convenience but lost the physical infrastructure designed for listening together. The shared experience of sound has been privatized, moving from the town square to the isolation of the noise-canceling …
```

**Summary:**
```
For many, the SWAIA Indian Market is Santa Fe’s most anticipated event of the year. Every August, over 1,000 celebrated artists from more than 200 communities, tribes, and nations gather in The City Different for the three-day event. This year marked the 103rd market, centered around the theme Iconic Beginnings: Legacies in Motion. SWAIA, the Southwest Association for Indian Arts, is an organization dedicated to supporting and creating economic opportunities for Native artists. What began as a small market and art fair in 1922 has now become the largest Native art show in the world. On Friday,…

### A Listener’s Guide to Chamber Music — with Dr. Paul Berry of Yale School of Music
**Publish Date:** 2025-01-13  
**Byline:** paige goehrig-bawany  
**Article Image:** https://ik.imagekit.io/odefm/chamber-instruments.jpg?updatedAt=1736735218871
[Read Article](https://ode.fm/article/listener-guide-chamber-music-dr-paul-berry-yale-school-music)

**Summary:**
```
What is Chamber Music? Chamber music is a genre of classical music written for small ensembles, typically performed without a conductor. Historically, it was played in private homes for intimate gatherings, but over time, it transitioned to larger venues and public performances. In its early days, chamber music was a social experience, with audiences often conversing while musicians played. As performances moved from homes to concert halls, composers adapted their work to capture the audience's attention in these new settings. For instance, the opening notes of pieces became more pronounced an…
```

Since Tchaikovsky’s death in 1893, The Sleeping Beauty has become one of the most famed ballets of all time. The ballet’s existential themes have resonated through time and space. “It was a form of giving, giving and taking. It was a kind of criticism against artists, who are always giving what they want to give. She kneels on stage accompanied only by a pair of scissors and invites the audience to come up one by one to cut away a piece of her clothing.
